# B200 port progress

This is the single canonical, sparse record of the B200 port. Keep detailed
logs, CSVs, PTX, SASS, and NCU reports under `.benchmarks/` and link them here.

| Date | Commit | Change or hypothesis | Verification and result | Next |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-08-28 | working tree | Established the starting B200 baseline. The Pixi environment sees compute capability 10.0; the torch-only 4096 BF16 benchmark completes, while current Ayla PTX still targets `sm_90a` and fails driver JIT on B200. | On an idle B200, `torch.mm` measured 0.146 ms / 944.390 TFLOP/s for 4096 cubed. Tests: 104 passed and 43 GPU tests failed at PTX module load; an NCU hardware-counter smoke test succeeded. | Add explicit architecture configuration and useful CUDA JIT diagnostics, then bring up a minimal B200 PTX kernel. |
| 2026-08-28 | working tree | Disabled the `fastcu_5_1` entry in the collection benchmark while retaining its embedded source. It is a Hopper-only `sm_90a`/`wgmma` reference generated from the currently unavailable fast.cu submodule, so it is not a valid B200 competitor. | `git diff --check` passes. | Re-enable only after replacing it with a valid Blackwell reference or making benchmark registration target-aware. |

| 2026-08-28 | `79b5b6b` | Batched four K=16 MMAs per commit and added a direct 128B-swizzled K=64 TMA path. On idle physical GPUs 2/6, direct TMA improved 0.916 ms → 0.201 ms at N=64 and 0.163 ms / 843.846 TFLOP/s at N=128; N=256 regressed to 0.188 ms ([raw](.benchmarks/2026-08-28-tcgen05-direct-tma-n256-gpu2.txt)). The extra issue/commit costs about 1.9% even with minimal duplicated output work, so terminal subdivision is rejected. | Return to one full-width instruction and audit whether a legal 16-bit accumulator can halve tensor-memory allocation and increase CTA residency. |

PTX/CUTLASS permit F16 `CFormat`, whose logical tensor-memory column stride is half FP32's, but the retained kernel's 109.568 KiB static shared memory still limits it to two CTAs/SM; producing BF16 would also require unpacked F16-to-BF16 conversions and would change accumulation accuracy. | Packed accumulation therefore cannot deliver the hypothesized residency gain in the current pipeline and has a predictably worse epilogue/accuracy tradeoff, so no invalid benchmark path was introduced. | Keep FP32 accumulation and re-test communication-free CTA clustering on the now-persistent one-wave launch, where scheduling differs from the earlier non-persistent experiment. |

The 256-block grid doubles independent CTAs relative to the retained N32 kernel while preserving one full-K batch and the common four-warp pipeline. | Seeds 42/43/44 were bit-exact, but N16 reached 78.341 TFLOP/s versus 88.127 retained and 125.028 torch on idle physical GPU 2 ([raw](.benchmarks/2026-08-30-tcgen05-512-n16-gpu2-forward.txt)). | Reject and remove N16 support: halving tensor instruction width costs 11.1%, so extra launch concurrency cannot close the small-shape gap. |

O2 K512 improved the same tile from 331.965 to 348.660 TFLOP/s, but remained below the retained M64/N64 K256 kernel at 372.181 and torch at 553.404 on idle physical GPU 2 ([raw](.benchmarks/2026-08-30-tcgen05-1024-m128n64-k512-gpu2-forward.txt)). | Reject the shape policy: deeper batching helps M128/N64 by 5.0%, but cannot overcome its half-sized block grid and generic epilogue. | 2026-08-30 | working tree | Prototyped SM100 `cta_group::2` TMA completion routing so both peer CTAs update the even CTA's barrier, targeting removal of the group-2 kernel's per-K cluster rendezvous. Reject and revert: peer-routed completion is promising only when it can safely replace cluster synchronization, and the current uniform protocol cannot do so across a multi-wave grid. |

Keep the 256-column allocation and pipeline the next K128 half-group across the prior full completion instead. |
| 2026-08-30 | working tree | Issued each next K128 group's first four MMAs and partial completion before polling the prior group's full completion, while delaying the second-bank TMA overwrite until after that poll. Restore native completion order and revisit peer-routed CTA-group-2 full/empty synchronization, the remaining mechanism with a measured latency signal. |
| 2026-08-30 | working tree | Implemented CUTLASS-style peer-routed CTA-group-2 TMA completion without a hot-loop cluster barrier. The barrier-free protocol is now understood, but group-2's mandatory safe allocation and half-per-CTA operand topology cannot approach the group-1 kernel without forbidden warp specialization. |

Batched and ordinary setup reached 915.614 and 914.831 TFLOP/s respectively versus 971.183 torch on idle physical GPU 2, a 0.09% difference inside noise ([raw](.benchmarks/2026-08-30-tcgen05-8192-batch-lock-init-gpu2-forward.txt)). | Reject the size-policy change: fixed initialization is already amortized across four outputs and does not affect the 8192 completion-bound critical path. |
